{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_07f881569b6642c4973c8660","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a2de082e-1fe7-4b26-af03-8881b9c6d955","uuid":"5bc8aec1-4369-4b45-bf6a-8287b1ba72cb","timestamp":"2026-11-04T11:15:54.656Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/8b3d7b600a02/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_07f881569b6642c4973c8660","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_67af3c291e3b4ed79bee54b4","type":"tool_result","content":"commit 056d436bf50c743cd2058cd5d3b95f6e0d20976e\nAuthor: loop-bench <loop-bench@localhost>\nDate:   Wed Nov 4 12:09:17 2026 +0100\n\n    test(tools): pin tool input validation contract\n    \n    Red acceptance tests for #37: ToolRegistry.execute must validate input\n    against the registered inputSchema before the executor runs, returning a\n    value-redacted ToolResponse rejection, compiling a static schema at\n    register, resolving function-valued sources per call, and feeding a\n    rejection back to the provider as a correctable tool result. message.content : []));\n41\t  return parts.filter((part): part is ToolResultContent => part.type === 'tool_result');\n42\t};\n43\t\n44\t// One scripted round per sendMessage call, recording the tool_result text each round is handed.\n45\tconst scriptedRounds = (rounds: MessageChunk[][]): {provider: AIProvider; toolResultTexts: string[]} => {\n46\t  const toolResultTexts: string[] = [];\n47\t  let round = 0;\n48\t  return {\n49\t    toolResultTexts,\n50\t    provider: {\n51\t      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n52\t        toolResultParts(params.messages).forEach(part => toolResultTexts.push(part.content));\n53\t        for (const chunk of rounds[round++] ?? []) yield chunk;\n54\t      },\n55\t      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n56\t        return {} as T;\n57\t      },\n58\t    },\n59\t  };\n60\t};\n61\t\n62\tconst TOOL_CALL: MessageChunk = {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}};\n63\tconst STOP_FOR_TOOL: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n64\tconst STOP_DONE: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n65\t\n66\tconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n67\t\n68\tdescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n69\t  it('returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor', async () => {\n70\t    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n71\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n72\t    registry.register(\n73\t      {\n74\t        name: 'get_weather',\n75\t        description: 'current weather for a location',\n76\t        inputSchema: {\n77\t          type: 'object',\n78\t          properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string', enum: ['celsius', 'fahrenheit']}},\n79\t          required: ['location'],\n80\t        },\n81\t      },\n82\t      run\n83\t    );\n84\t\n85\t    const missingLocation = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n86\t    const offMenuUnits = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin', units: offMenuUnits.result.includes('units'),\n92\t    }).toEqual({executed: 0, namesMissing: true, namesUnits: true});\n93\t  });\n94\t\n95\t  it('passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra const logged: string[] = [];\n113\t    const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string) => void logged.push(`${level} ${message}`);\n114\t    setLogger({debug: record('DEBUG'), info: record('INFO'), warn: record('WARN'), error: record('ERROR')});\n115\t    let response: ToolResponse;\n116\t    try {\n117\t      response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 424242, requestedFor: 'patient-778899'});\n118\t    } finally {\n119\t      setLogger();\n120\t    }\n121\t\n122\t    const seen = [response.result, ...logged].join('\\n');\n123\t    expect({\n124\t seen.includes('patient-778899'),\n128\t    }).toEqual({executed: 0, namesPath: true, namesType: true, valueRedacted: false});\n129\t  });\n130\t\n131\t  it('throws at register when the input schema {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n140\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n141\t    let currentSchema = locationSchema();\n142\t    registry.register(() => weatherTool(currentSchema), run);\n143\t\n144\t    const first = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n145\t    currentSchema = {type: 'object'};\n146\t    await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n147\t\n148\t    expect({executed: calls.length, firstNamesLocation: first.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n149\t      executed: 1,\n150\t      firstNamesLocation: true,\n151\t    });\n152\t  });\n153\t\n154\t  it('keeps validating a static tool against the schema it was registered with', async () => {\n155\t    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n156\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n157\t    const registeredSchema = locationSchema();\n158\t    registry.register(weatherTool(registeredSchema), run);\n159\t    // Loosened after register: only a schema compiled at register time can ignore response.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n165\t      executed: 0,\n166\t      namesLocation: true,\n167\t    });\n168\t  });\n169\t\n170\t  // Pins the one failure the ticket keeps as a throw rather than a returned rejection.\n171\t  it('still throws Unknown tool for a name that was never registered', async () => {\n172\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n173\t\n174\t    await expect(registry.execute('ghost_tool', {})).rejects.toThrow('Unknown tool: ghost_tool');\n175\t  });\n176\t});\n177\t\n178\tdescribe('input validation through the orchestrator tool loop', () => {\n179\t  it('returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it', async () => {\n180\t    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n181\t    const registry = new [{type: 'text', text: '18 degrees in Berlin'}, STOP_DONE],\n186\t    ]);\n187\t    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n188\t\n189\t    const text = await orchestrator.processMessage(session('weather in Berlin?'));\n190\t\n191\t    expect({resolved: text, delivered: toolResultTexts, executed: calls.length}).toEqual({\n192\t      resolved: '18 degrees in });\n197\t});\n198\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a2de082e-1fe7-4b26-af03-8881b9c6d955","uuid":"03caf8c4-7972-4455-aad3-23a9f139b624","timestamp":"2026-11-04T11:16:21.272Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/8b3d7b600a02/workspace/target/src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts","content":"// Acceptance tests for tool input validation — the contract lives in specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md.\nimport {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, Message, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams, ToolResultContent} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\nimport {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport type {ToolExecutor, ToolResponse} from './registry.js';\n\n// The calls an executor actually received, so a test can assert what reached it.\nconst recordingExecutor = (): {calls: Record<string, unknown>[]; run: ToolExecutor} => {\n  const calls: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  const run: ToolExecutor = async input => {\n    calls.push(input);\n    return 'tool ran';\n  };\n  return {calls, run};\n};\n\n// Fresh schema per test: two of them mutate or replace the object after register.\nconst locationSchema = (): Record<string, unknown> => ({\n  type: 'object',\n  properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n  required: ['location'],\n});\n\nconst weatherTool = (inputSchema: Record<string, unknown>) => ({\n  name: 'get_weather',\n  description: 'current weather for a location',\n  inputSchema,\n});\n\nconst session = (content: string): ChatSession => ({\n  sessionId: 's1',\n  userId: 'u1',\n  entries: [{role: 'user', content, timestamp: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'}],\n});\n\nconst toolResultParts = (messages: Message[]): ToolResultContent[] => {\n  const parts = messages.flatMap(message => (Array.isArray(message.content) ? message.content : []));\n  return parts.filter((part): part is ToolResultContent => part.type === 'tool_result');\n};\n\n// One scripted round per sendMessage call, recording the tool_result text each round is handed.\nconst scriptedRounds = (rounds: MessageChunk[][]): {provider: AIProvider; toolResultTexts: string[]} => {\n  const toolResultTexts: string[] = [];\n  let round = 0;\n  return {\n    toolResultTexts,\n    provider: {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        toolResultParts(params.messages).forEach(part => toolResultTexts.push(part.content));\n        for (const chunk of rounds[round++] ?? []) yield chunk;\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    },\n  };\n};\n\nconst TOOL_CALL: MessageChunk = {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}};\nconst STOP_FOR_TOOL: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\nconst STOP_DONE: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n\nconst promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'});\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry input validation', () => {\n  it('returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'get_weather',\n        description: 'current weather for a location',\n        inputSchema: {\n          type: 'object',\n          properties: {location: {type: 'string'}, units: {type: 'string', enum: ['celsius', 'fahrenheit']}},\n          required: ['location'],\n        },\n      },\n      run\n    );\n\n    const missingLocation = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n    const offMenuUnits = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin', units: 'kelvin'});\n\n    expect({\n      executed: calls.length,\n      namesMissing: missingLocation.result.includes('location'),\n      namesUnits: offMenuUnits.result.includes('units'),\n    }).toEqual({executed: 0, namesMissing: true, namesUnits: true});\n  });\n\n  it('passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra properties included', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(weatherTool(locationSchema()), run);\n\n    await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 'Berlin', units: 'metric'});\n\n    expect({executed: calls.length, received: calls[0]}).toEqual({\n      executed: 1,\n      received: {location: 'Berlin', units: 'metric'},\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(weatherTool(locationSchema()), run);\n    const logged: string[] = [];\n    const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string) => void logged.push(`${level} ${message}`);\n    setLogger({debug: record('DEBUG'), info: record('INFO'), warn: record('WARN'), error: record('ERROR')});\n    let response: ToolResponse;\n    try {\n      response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 424242, requestedFor: 'patient-778899'});\n    } finally {\n      setLogger();\n    }\n\n    const seen = [response.result, ...logged].join('\\n');\n    expect({\n      executed: calls.length,\n      namesPath: response.result.includes('location'),\n      namesType: response.result.includes('string'),\n      valueRedacted: seen.includes('424242') || seen.includes('patient-778899'),\n    }).toEqual({executed: 0, namesPath: true, namesType: true, valueRedacted: false});\n  });\n\n  it('throws at register when the input schema cannot compile, naming the tool', () => {\n    const {run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n\n    expect(() => registry.register(weatherTool({type: 'objct', properties: {}}), run)).toThrow(/get_weather/);\n  });\n\n  it('validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let currentSchema = locationSchema();\n    registry.register(() => weatherTool(currentSchema), run);\n\n    const first = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n    currentSchema = {type: 'object'};\n    await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect({executed: calls.length, firstNamesLocation: first.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      executed: 1,\n      firstNamesLocation: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('keeps validating a static tool against the schema it was registered with', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const registeredSchema = locationSchema();\n    registry.register(weatherTool(registeredSchema), run);\n    // Loosened after register: only a schema compiled at register time can ignore the edit.\n    registeredSchema.required = [];\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect({executed: calls.length, namesLocation: response.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      executed: 0,\n      namesLocation: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  // Pins the one failure the ticket keeps as a throw rather than a returned rejection.\n  it('still throws Unknown tool for a name that was never registered', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n\n    await expect(registry.execute('ghost_tool', {})).rejects.toThrow('Unknown tool: ghost_tool');\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('input validation through the orchestrator tool loop', () => {\n  it('returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it', async () => {\n    const {calls, run} = recordingExecutor();\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(weatherTool(locationSchema()), run);\n    const {provider, toolResultTexts} = scriptedRounds([\n      [TOOL_CALL, STOP_FOR_TOOL],\n      [{type: 'text', text: '18 degrees in Berlin'}, STOP_DONE],\n    ]);\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n\n    const text = await orchestrator.processMessage(session('weather in Berlin?'));\n\n    expect({resolved: text, delivered: toolResultTexts, executed: calls.length}).toEqual({\n      resolved: '18 degrees in Berlin',\n      delivered: [expect.stringContaining('location')],\n      executed: 0,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":198,"startLine":1,"totalLines":198}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_8a11aa09d64a4e3c9a8588fa","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_b10508ac11fa40a2a99c747d","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Tool Input Validation\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n6\t| Status | Shipped              |\n7\t\n8\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is handed verbatim to the providers, so the model knows the shape of a call. Nothing checked that the call it actually sent matched. This spec states what the registry enforces between the two: a call the schema rejects fails in one predictable way, returned as a tool result the model can read and correct inside the existing tool-round budget, and a call the schema accepts reaches the executor exactly as it arrived.\n9\t\n10\t## The contract\n11\t\n12\t- `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema`\n13\t  before the executor runs\n14\t  ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n15\t  [validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L179)).\n16\t- A call the schema rejects is returned as a `ToolResponse` whose `result` describes the failure,\n17\t  rather than thrown\n18\t  ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n19\t  [validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L179)).\n20\t- The executor is never invoked for input the schema rejects\n21\t  ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n22\t  [validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108)).\n23\t- Validation enforces the schema's `required` list, declared property types and `enum` values, and\n24\t  nothing beyond them\n25\t  ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n26\t  [validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108),\n27\t  [validated by: passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra properties included](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L95)).\n28\t- A rejection message names the failing property path and the constraint that failed, and carries no\n29\t  property value\n30\t  ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108)).\n31\t- No log line written while a rejected call is handled carries an input value\n32\t  ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108)).\n33\t- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged -- unlisted properties included, nothing\n34\t  injected or coerced\n35\t  ([validated by: passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra properties included](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L95)).\n36\t- A static tool's schema is compiled once at `register`, so a schema that cannot compile fails at\n37\t  registration with an error naming the tool\n38\t  ([validated by: throws at register when the input schema cannot compile, naming the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L131),\n39\t  [validated by: keeps validating a static tool against the schema it was registered with](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L154)).\n40\t- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` is resolved per call and validated against the schema\n41\t  current at that call\n42\t  ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L138)).\n43\t- A rejected tool call reaches the provider as a tool result on the next round, so `processMessage`\n44\t  resolves and the model can correct the call within the tool-round budget\n45\t  ([validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L179)).\n46\t- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name\n47\t  ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for a name that was never registered](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L171)).\n48\t\n49\t## Out of scope\n50\t\n51\t- There is no opt-out flag: every registered tool is validated, and no per-tool or per-call switch\n52\t  turns it off.\n53\t- An unregistered name keeps its existing `Unknown tool: <name>` throw -- the same failure class\n54\t  handled the opposite way, an intentional split tracked separately.\n55\t- Validating tool *output*, hallucination detection and an evaluation harness, per-tool\n56\t  authorization (a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still valid), and retry, timeout\n57\t  and rate limiting around tool execution are separate concerns.\n58\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a2de082e-1fe7-4b26-af03-8881b9c6d955","uuid":"e3aeee3d-cd0b-4dbe-9323-8d4809b6e17e","timestamp":"2026-11-04T11:16:34.174Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/8b3d7b600a02/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n  [validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L179)).\n- The executor is never invoked for input the schema rejects\n  ([validated path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108)).\n- Validation enforces the schema's `required` list, declared property types and `enum` values, and\n  nothing beyond them\n  ([validated by: returns a rejection naming what the schema rejected and skips the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L69),\n  [validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108),\n  [validated by: passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra properties included](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L95)).\n- A rejection message names the failing property path and the constraint naming the path and expected type, redacting the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L108)).\n- Input the schema accepts reaches the executor unchanged -- unlisted properties included, nothing\n  injected or coerced\n  ([validated by: passes accepted input to the executor unchanged, extra properties included](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L95)).\n- A static tool's schema is compiled once at `register`, so a schema that cannot compile fails at\n  registration with an error naming the tool\n  ([validated by: throws at register when the input schema cannot compile, naming the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L131),\n  [validated by: keeps validating a static tool against the schema it was registered with](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L154)).\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` is resolved per call and validated against the schema\n  current at that call\n  ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L138)).\n- A rejected tool call reaches the provider as a tool result on the next round, so `processMessage`\n  resolves and the model can correct the call within the tool-round budget\n  ([validated by: returns a rejected tool call to the provider so the next round can correct it](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L179)).\n- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name\n  ([validated by: still throws Unknown tool for a name that was never registered](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L171)).\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- There is no opt-out flag: every registered tool is validated, and no per-tool or per-call switch\n  turns it off.\n- An unregistered name keeps its existing `Unknown tool: <name>` throw -- the same failure class\n  handled the opposite way, an intentional split tracked separately.\n- Validating tool *output*, hallucination detection and an evaluation harness, per-tool\n  authorization (a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still valid), and retry, timeout\n  and rate limiting around tool execution are separate concerns.\n","numLines":58,"startLine":1,"totalLines":58}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_b10508ac11fa40a2a99c747d","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{result: response} : response;\n}\n\nexport type ToolDefinitionSource = ToolDefinition | (() => ToolDefinition);\n\nexport interface RegisteredTool {\n  definition: ToolDefinition;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\ninterface StoredTool {\n  definitionSource: ToolDefinitionSource;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\nexport class ToolRegistry {\n  private readonly tools = new Map<string, StoredTool>();\n\n  private resolveDefinition(source: ToolDefinitionSource): ToolDefinition {\n    return typeof source === 'function' ? source() : source;\n  }\n\n  register(definition: ToolDefinitionSource, execute: ToolExecutor): void {\n    const resolved = this.resolveDefinition(definition);\n    this.tools.set(resolved.name, {definitionSource: definition, execute});\n  }\n\n  get(name: string): RegisteredTool | undefined {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) return undefined;\n    return {definition: this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource), execute: tool.execute};\n  }\n\n  getDefinitions(): ToolDefinition[] {\n    return [...this.tools.values()].map(t => this.resolveDefinition(t.definitionSource));\n  }\n\n  getPromptInstructions(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions()\n      .filter(t => t.promptInstructions)\n      .map(t => `${t.name}: ${t.promptInstructions}`);\n  }\n\n  getExamplePrompts(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions().flatMap(t => t.examplePrompts ?? []);\n  }\n\n  has(name: string): boolean {\n    return this.tools.has(name);\n  }\n\n  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n    }\n    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n      name,\n      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ??
